		<item>
		<title>Single-shot and agentic retrieval with Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/single-shot-and-agentic-retrieval-with-amazon-bedrock-managed-knowledge-base/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Siddhant Sahu]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 09 Nov 2026 20:13:18 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Expert (400)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">85e11029965ec714b8161f63f048a12f65fd6a97</guid>

					<description>Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base now supports single-shot RAG and retrieval-only requests through AgenticRetrieveStream, alongside agentic retrieval. This post explains how the maxAgentIteration parameter matches retrieval effort to each request, using an illustrative internal banking policy assistant.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;A banking operations employee starts the morning by asking an internal AI assistant which documents are needed to open a business account. The answer is in a single checklist. A few hours later, the same employee asks how those requirements differ across sole proprietorships, LLCs, and partnerships with multiple owners. That answer is spread across several policy guides. One assistant has to handle both questions, quickly and cheaply for the first and thoroughly for the second. Applications need a way to match retrieval effort to each request without building separate systems for simple and complex questions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base now supports single-shot Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) and retrieval-only requests through &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt;. With single-shot RAG, the service searches your knowledge bases once and uses a foundation model (FM) to generate an answer from the passages it finds. With retrieval-only requests, the service returns the matching passages without generating an answer, so your application or the user can work with the source text directly. These modes join the existing agentic retrieval, where the service plans and runs multiple searches before answering. You can now use the same API for both of the employee’s questions: single-shot RAG for the checklist lookup and agentic retrieval for the cross-policy comparison.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we explain how the new settings work and when to use each mode. We use an illustrative internal banking policy assistant to show how single-shot RAG handles direct questions and how agentic retrieval supports comparisons across policy documents. We also show how to configure retrieval-only requests when an employee needs to review source passages rather than a generated answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="key-benefits"&gt;Key benefits&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-test-agentic-retrieve.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Agentic retrieval&lt;/a&gt; helps an assistant answer questions that require more than one search. A foundation model breaks the question into sub-queries, searches the relevant knowledge bases, and evaluates whether the results provide enough information to answer the original question. If it identifies gaps, it can refine the search and retrieve additional evidence, within the configured iteration limit.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For application builders, this provides several benefits:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Better support for multi-part questions –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;Targeted searches can find evidence for each part of a question instead of relying on one search to cover everything. In the banking example, the assistant can look up requirements for sole proprietors and LLCs separately before comparing them.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Follow-up searches when information is missing –&lt;/strong&gt; The planner can use what it finds to decide what to search for next. It can also retrieve a full document when a passage doesn’t provide enough context, helping address gaps that a single retrieval might leave unresolved.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Answers with supporting evidence –&lt;/strong&gt;&amp;nbsp;When response generation is enabled, the service combines retrieved information into an answer with citations. Trace events show the planning and retrieval steps, helping developers inspect how the answer was produced.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Less custom orchestration –&lt;/strong&gt; The service manages the planning, retrieval, evaluation, and response-generation workflow through one API. Applications don’t need to implement their own loop to coordinate these steps.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These agentic capabilities remain valuable for complex questions. For straightforward lookups, applications also need a way to retrieve and answer without additional planning. The expanded &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration&lt;/code&gt; range closes that gap. With one parameter, you can choose one of three modes for each request:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Retrieval-only&lt;/strong&gt; returns passages without generating an answer.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Single-shot RAG&lt;/strong&gt; retrieves once and generates an answer, with no planning rounds.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Agentic retrieval&lt;/strong&gt; plans and runs multiple searches for complex questions.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Single-shot RAG uses one foundation model call to generate the answer, which can reduce latency and model usage compared with agentic retrieval. Retrieval-only requests make no foundation model calls. You can balance speed and answer quality for each request, without integrating a second API or maintaining separate code paths for simple and complex queries.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-retrieval-iterations-are-counted"&gt;How retrieval iterations are counted&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before walking through the new values, it helps to anchor on how iterations are counted, because that is what makes the behavior predictable.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;One iteration equals one foundation model call. The loop always ends with a single answer-generation call. A value of &lt;em&gt;n&lt;/em&gt; buys up to &lt;em&gt;n&lt;/em&gt; minus 1 agentic planning rounds, with the last iteration reserved for generating the response. Each planning round is one query rewrite plus a retrieval. More iterations means more planning rounds and better results on hard questions. Fewer iterations means lower latency.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;One thing that doesn’t&amp;nbsp;count: speculative retrieval. The initial retrieval that runs ahead of the first planning step is retrieval, not an iteration. It never counts against your maxAgentIteration value, and it only ever makes a request faster. Keep that distinction in mind as you read the following values.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="whats-new"&gt;What’s new&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The range is now 0–5.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;All three modes support multiple knowledge bases, including &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=0&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;1&lt;/code&gt;. Single-shot retrieval doesn’t mean a single knowledge base. Agentic mode adds query decomposition, evidence evaluation, and iterative searches, rather than exclusive access to multiple knowledge bases.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="maxagentiteration-1-basic-rag-the-headline-use-case"&gt;maxAgentIteration = 1: Basic RAG (the headline use case)&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This is single-shot RAG: one retrieval, then one answer. No agentic planning, no query rewriting. The API retrieves once against your query and generates a response grounded in those passages. This is the &lt;em&gt;retrieve once, then generate a response&lt;/em&gt; pattern that previously wasn’t expressible through this API.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Pair it with &lt;code&gt;generateResponse=true&lt;/code&gt; to get a generated answer grounded in the retrieved passages. If you only need the passages, use &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=0&lt;/code&gt; instead, as described in the next section.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If most of your traffic is well-scoped, direct questions, this is likely the setting you want as a default.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="maxagentiteration-0-passages-only-zero-llm-calls"&gt;maxAgentIteration = 0: Passages only, zero LLM calls&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This runs one guaranteed retrieval and generates nothing. No foundation model is invoked. It is equivalent to calling the Retrieve API directly. This makes it most useful for callers who are already standardized on &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt; and would rather change a parameter than switch APIs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Because no large language model (LLM) is called, there is no answer to generate. Setting &lt;code&gt;generateResponse=true&lt;/code&gt; at this value is invalid and returns a 4xx error, because generating an answer requires an LLM call.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="maxagentiteration-2-to-5-agentic-mode-unchanged"&gt;maxAgentIteration = 2 to 5: Agentic mode, unchanged&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/kb-test-agentic-retrieve.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;agentic mode&lt;/a&gt;, a foundation model breaks complex questions into focused sub-queries and retrieves evidence in multiple steps, including across multiple knowledge bases. It evaluates the retrieved information and can refine its searches when evidence is missing. This approach can improve retrieval accuracy for multi-part and comparative questions that a single search might not fully address.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With response generation enabled, a value of &lt;em&gt;n&lt;/em&gt; allows up to &lt;em&gt;n&lt;/em&gt; minus 1 planning rounds before generating an answer grounded in the retrieved evidence. Existing requests using values from 2–5 retain their current behavior.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="choosing-a-value"&gt;Choosing a value&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;One way to reason about it:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What you get&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Best for&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;One retrieval, no generation, zero LLM calls&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Passage retrieval. Callers standardized on this API who want Retrieve-style behavior&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;1&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;One retrieval plus one answer&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Single-shot RAG for direct, well-scoped questions&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;2 to 5&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Up to &lt;em&gt;n&lt;/em&gt; minus 1 planning rounds, then an answer&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Multi-part, comparative, exploratory, and cross-knowledge-base questions&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The lower the value, the faster and more predictable the call. The higher the value, the more the planner can decompose and refine on genuinely hard questions. Start low for simple traffic, raise it when accuracy on complex queries matters more than latency.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="in-practice-an-internal-banking-policy-assistant"&gt;In practice: An internal banking policy assistant&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Consider a fictional bank whose operations team uses an internal assistant backed by a knowledge base of account-opening procedures, documentation checklists, and policy guides. Employees use the assistant to locate and understand the bank’s documented requirements. The following examples illustrate how the application can select a retrieval mode for each workflow. They don’t describe a customer deployment or measured results.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows how the banking assistant selects a retrieval mode for each request.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-22223-1-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-22223-1-1.png" alt="The banking assistant routes each question to AgenticRetrieveStream in single-shot, agentic, or retrieval-only mode" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: How the banking assistant selects a retrieval mode for each request&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;An employee asks the assistant a question. The application chooses a retrieval mode and sends the request to &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt;. With single-shot RAG, the service searches the knowledge base once and generates an answer. With agentic retrieval, the service splits the question into parts, searches several times, and then generates an answer with citations. With retrieval-only, the service returns source passages without generating an answer. The following sections explain each mode.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="answer-a-direct-documentation-question"&gt;Answer a direct documentation question&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;An employee asks, “What documents are required to open a business checking account for a sole proprietor?” In this example, the bank maintains a dedicated checklist that contains the answer. The application sets &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=1&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;generateResponse=true&lt;/code&gt; to retrieve relevant passages and generate a response, without query rewriting or agentic planning.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This is the single-shot RAG pattern: one retrieval, followed by one answer-generation call. It avoids a planning step when the question and supporting content are already well scoped.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="compare-requirements-across-policy-documents"&gt;Compare requirements across policy documents&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Another employee asks, “How do account-opening requirements differ for sole proprietors and LLCs, and what additional checks, if any, apply to nonresident owners under our policy?” Answering this question might require information from separate procedures and ownership-verification guides.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For this workflow, the application can set &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=4&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;generateResponse=true&lt;/code&gt;, allowing up to three planning rounds before generating an answer. The planner can break the question into sub-queries, retrieve relevant evidence, and refine its search if information is missing. The appropriate iteration limit should be evaluated against representative questions rather than assuming that a higher value always produces a better answer.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="show-source-passages-without-generating-an-answer"&gt;Show source passages without generating an answer&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;An employee reviewing an account-opening procedure might want the relevant policy text instead of a generated summary. The application sets &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=0&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;generateResponse=false&lt;/code&gt; to return passages with no language model calls. It can then display those passages and their source references for the employee to review.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;All three workflows use &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt;. The application chooses the iteration limit and whether to return a generated answer. Selecting the mode is application logic, not automatic routing provided by this parameter. The assistant supports policy lookup and comparison. It doesn’t approve accounts or replace the bank’s review and authorization processes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following example shows a complete agentic request. The sections after it adapt it for single-shot RAG and retrieval-only modes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="select-a-retrieval-mode-for-the-banking-assistant"&gt;Select a retrieval mode for the banking assistant&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The sample code demonstrates the three workflows using fictional policy documents.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;

{
    "messages": [
        { 
            "content": { 
                "text": "How do account-opening requirements differ for sole proprietors and LLCs, and what additional checks, if any, apply to nonresident owners under our policy?" 
            }, 
            "role": "user" 
        }
    ],
    "retrievers": [
        {
            "description": "Knowledge base of account-opening procedures, documentation checklists, and policy guides",
            "configuration": { 
                "knowledgeBase": { 
                    "knowledgeBaseId": "KB12345678" 
                } 
            }
        }
    ],
    "generateResponse": true,
    "agenticRetrieveConfiguration": {
        "foundationModelType": "MANAGED",
        "rerankingModelType": "MANAGED",
        "maxAgentIteration": 4
    }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="example-single-shot-rag"&gt;Example: Single-shot RAG&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Setting up basic RAG is a one-line change from an agentic call. Set &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration&lt;/code&gt; to 1 and keep response generation on.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;

{
    "messages": [
        { 
            "content": { 
                "text": "What documents are required to open a business checking account for a sole proprietor?" 
            }, 
            "role": "user" 
        }
    ],
    "retrievers": [
        {
            "description": "Knowledge base of account-opening procedures, documentation checklists, and policy guides",
            "configuration": { 
                "knowledgeBase": { 
                    "knowledgeBaseId": "KB12345678" 
                } 
            }
        }
    ],
    "generateResponse": true,
    "agenticRetrieveConfiguration": {
        "foundationModelType": "MANAGED",
        "rerankingModelType": "MANAGED",
        "maxAgentIteration": 1
    }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You consume the stream the same way you always have. The difference is what’s in it: a single retrieval and one grounded answer, with no planning trace events in between.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="example-passages-only"&gt;Example: Passages only&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For retrieval with no generation, set &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration&lt;/code&gt; to 0. Response generation must be off at this value.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;

{
    "messages": [
        { 
            "content": { 
                "text": "Show the policy text for the business account-opening procedure." 
            }, 
            "role": "user" 
        }
    ],
    "retrievers": [
        {
            "description": "Knowledge base of account-opening procedures, documentation checklists, and policy guides",
            "configuration": { 
                "knowledgeBase": { 
                    "knowledgeBaseId": "KB12345678" 
                } 
            }
        }
    ],
    "generateResponse": false,
    "agenticRetrieveConfiguration": {
        "foundationModelType": "MANAGED",
        "rerankingModelType": "MANAGED",
        "maxAgentIteration": 0
    }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This returns the deduplicated source passages with no generated answer. The retrieval behavior matches a direct Retrieve call, but the response format differs: passages arrive in the stream’s &lt;code&gt;result&lt;/code&gt; event without &lt;code&gt;score&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;location&lt;/code&gt; fields.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base, you can now combine single-shot RAG, agentic retrieval, and retrieval-only requests in one assistant through &lt;code&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream&lt;/code&gt;. In the banking example, you can answer the account-opening checklist question with &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=1&lt;/code&gt; and response generation enabled, which produces an answer without planning calls. For the comparison across business structures, you can set &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=4&lt;/code&gt; to allow additional searches before the response is generated. When an employee needs to read the policy text, you can set &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration=0&lt;/code&gt; with response generation disabled to return passages without calling a language model. Your application selects the mode, and employees keep using the same assistant.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To adopt the new modes in an existing application, start with a representative set of questions and expected answers from your own documents. Compare single-shot RAG with your current agentic configuration using the same knowledge base. Measure response latency and request cost, and check whether answers are correct and supported by the retrieved passages. Include questions whose answers span several documents, and questions the documents can’t answer, so a faster response doesn’t hide missing evidence or unsupported conclusions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Use those results to choose the lowest iteration limit that meets each workflow’s quality requirements. Keep agentic retrieval where additional searches improve the answer, use single-shot RAG where one retrieval is sufficient, and disable generation when the task is source review. Existing requests with values from 2–5 remain unchanged, so you can introduce the new modes selectively rather than redesigning the entire application.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, configure &lt;code&gt;maxAgentIteration&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;generateResponse&lt;/code&gt; in your requests using the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/APIReference/API_agent-runtime_AgenticRetrieveStream.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgenticRetrieveStream API reference&lt;/a&gt;, then evaluate the settings against your application’s questions and response-time targets. To learn more about the service, visit the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/knowledge-bases/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Managed Knowledge Base&lt;/a&gt; service page.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt;

					<description>Claude Fable 5.2 and Claude Mythos 5.2 are now available on Amazon Bedrock and Claude Platform on AWS. Learn what's new in both models, why Fable 5.2 matters for your technical workloads, and how to get started building with it on Amazon Bedrock.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Today, we are announcing the availability of Claude Fable 5.2 and Claude Mythos 5.2 on Amazon Bedrock and Claude Platform on AWS. Claude Fable 5.2 is Anthropic’s most capable generally available model for technical work that raises the bar in coding and the sciences. Claude Mythos 5.2 is available through Anthropic’s verification programs for advanced work in cyber security and life sciences.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we cover what’s new in both models, why Fable 5.2 matters for your technical workloads, and how to get started building with the model on Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="why-claude-fable-5.2"&gt;Why Claude Fable 5.2&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Fable 5.2 raises the bar on the hardest problems in coding, math, and science. It takes on ambiguous, multi-step work, verifies its own output, and can run unattended for hours to days. It interprets prompts, iterates, catches its own errors, and optimizes as it goes.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In Anthropic’s alignment testing, Mythos 5.2 hallucinates less, completes tasks more reliably, surfaces important information more consistently, and self-reports issues more honestly than Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, and Mythos 5.1. Anthropic expects Fable 5.2 to behave similarly.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If a cyber security query is declined, you can retry it on Opus 4.8, and if a biology query is declined, you can retry it on Opus 5. On Amazon Bedrock, the retry is your code’s job, not an automatic switch. If the declined request hit the prompt cache, the refusal also includes a fallback credit token. Send that token with the retry and it’s billed as cache reads, so you don’t pay to write the same cache twice. Check the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/claude-messages-fallback-credit.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;documentation&lt;/a&gt; for more information on fallback credit.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For authorized work in cyber security and life sciences, Claude Mythos 5.2 is available through &lt;a href="https://www.anthropic.com/news/cyber-verification-program" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Anthropic’s verification programs&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="claude-fable-5.2-and-mythos-5.2-use-cases"&gt;Claude Fable 5.2 and Mythos 5.2 use cases&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Where Fable 5.2 fits:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Agentic coding&lt;/strong&gt; across large, multi-repository codebases.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Long-running agents&lt;/strong&gt; for coordinating many subagents on multi-day work.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scientific research&lt;/strong&gt; from new analytical methods to formal mathematical proofs.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Financial analysis&lt;/strong&gt; for quantitative research and forecasting.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Design for 3D, hardware, and motion&lt;/strong&gt; for generating environments, components, and simulations in code.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Mythos 5.2 covers two high-risk domains, available only through the verification programs noted earlier:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cyber security&lt;/strong&gt;, for security engineering, red teaming, and penetration testing.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Life sciences research&lt;/strong&gt;, especially biomolecular design and research synthesis.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="data-retention-and-enterprise-frontier-safeguards"&gt;Data retention and Enterprise Frontier Safeguards&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Anthropic designates Claude Fable 5.2 as a &lt;a href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15425695-covered-models" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Covered Model&lt;/a&gt;. By default, its use requires retention for up to 30 days and human safety review. On Amazon Bedrock, Amazon personnel perform the review within the AWS boundary. Set the &lt;code&gt;aws_review&lt;/code&gt; data retention mode on your account or project before you invoke the model. See &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/data-retention.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock data retention&lt;/a&gt; for more details.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;span&gt;&lt;a href="https://www.anthropic.com/news/enterprise-frontier-safeguards" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"&gt;Enterprise Frontier Safeguards&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/span&gt; (EFS), built in collaboration with AWS, combines the privacy of zero data retention (ZDR) with state-of-the-art safeguards for detecting misuse. EFS works by storing data in cloud infrastructure controlled by the customer. Until EFS is available, eligible customers can use Fable 5.2 with zero data retention. This is available for internal use through at least June 30, 2027.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="getting-started-with-claude-fable-5.2-on-amazon-bedrock"&gt;Getting started with Claude Fable 5.2 on Amazon Bedrock&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To try Fable 5.2, open the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock console&lt;/a&gt;, choose &lt;strong&gt;Test&lt;/strong&gt;, &lt;strong&gt;Playground&lt;/strong&gt;, and select &lt;strong&gt;Fable 5.2&lt;/strong&gt; as the model. From there, you can run a prompt directly against it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Programmatically, you can call the model with the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/model-parameters-anthropic-claude-messages.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Anthropic Messages API&lt;/a&gt; against &lt;code&gt;bedrock-runtime&lt;/code&gt; (through the Anthropic SDK). You can also stay on the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/inference-api.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Invoke&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/conversation-inference.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Converse&lt;/a&gt; APIs on &lt;code&gt;bedrock-runtime&lt;/code&gt; through the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cli/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Command Line Interface (AWS CLI)&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/developer/tools/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS SDK&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To follow along with the code examples in this post, you need the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An active &lt;a href="https://portal.aws.amazon.com/gp/aws/developer/registration/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS account&lt;/a&gt; with Amazon Bedrock access.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cli/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CLI&lt;/a&gt; installed and configured with appropriate credentials.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.9 or later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://boto3.amazonaws.com/v1/documentation/api/latest/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Boto3&lt;/a&gt; (AWS SDK for Python) installed: &lt;code&gt;pip install boto3&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) permissions: &lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModel&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModelWithResponseStream&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Here’s a quick example using the AWS SDK for Python (Boto3):&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import boto3
import json

# Create a Bedrock Runtime client
bedrock_runtime = boto3.client(
    service_name="bedrock-runtime",
    region_name="us-east-1"
)

# Invoke Claude Fable 5.2
response = bedrock_runtime.invoke_model(
    modelId="global.anthropic.claude-fable-5-2",
    contentType="application/json",
    accept="application/json",
    body=json.dumps({
        "anthropic_version": "bedrock-2023-05-31",
        "max_tokens": 4096,
        "messages": [
            {
                "role": "user",
                "content": "An S3 bucket serves 40 TB/month egress. Estimate the monthly egress cost at $0.09/GB, and state one architecture change, using AWS services, to cut it. Show the calculation, keep it under 120 words."
            }
        ]
    })
)

result = json.loads(response["body"].read())

# Fable 5.2 is a reasoning model: the response may include a thinking block
# before the text block, so select the text block rather than a fixed index.
print(next(b["text"] for b in result["content"] if b["type"] == "text"))&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can explore the &lt;u&gt;&lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/anthropic-on-aws/blob/main/notebooks/claude_fable_5_2_getting_started/claude-fable-5-2-getting-started.ipynb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"&gt;Getting Started notebook&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/u&gt; for more examples.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="migrating-to-claude-fable-5.2-with-the-claude-api-skill-in-claude-code"&gt;Migrating to Claude Fable 5.2 with the claude-api skill in Claude Code&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For teams already running Claude models on Amazon Bedrock, the claude-api skill in Claude Code helps you upgrade to Fable 5.2.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The claude-api skill is an open-source skill built into &lt;a href="https://code.claude.com/docs" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Claude Code&lt;/a&gt; that captures the details that make Claude API code work well. It covers which agent patterns fit a given job, what parameters change between model generations, and when to apply prompt caching. It stays current as Anthropic’s SDKs evolve: when a new model is released or the API gains a feature, the skill already knows.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="what-the-skill-does-for-fable-5.2-migrations"&gt;What the skill does for Fable 5.2 migrations&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you ask Claude Code to upgrade your codebase to Fable 5.2, the skill reviews your code and walks you through:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Updating model references&lt;/strong&gt; swapping model IDs to the Fable 5.2 ID across your application code.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Adjusting thinking settings&lt;/strong&gt; moving manual thinking configurations to adaptive thinking where appropriate for the new model.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cleaning up outdated parameters&lt;/strong&gt; removing deprecated beta headers, parameters, and settings that no longer apply.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Suggesting effort levels&lt;/strong&gt; recommending the right &lt;code&gt;output_config.effort&lt;/code&gt; settings inline based on your use case.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The skill also detects your deployment platform including Amazon Bedrock, Claude Platform on AWS, and other environments and adjusts model ID formats and feature configurations accordingly. For example, it uses the Amazon Bedrock structured model ID format (&lt;code&gt;global.anthropic.claude-fable-5-2&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="how-to-use-it"&gt;How to use it&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In Claude Code, run the migration directly:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;/claude-api migrate&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This scans your working directory (or a subdirectory or specific file list you confirm) and applies the migration changes in a single guided pass. You can also invoke it conversationally by telling Claude Code:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;em&gt;“Upgrade me to Claude Fable 5.2”&lt;/em&gt; Claude reviews your code and walks through the necessary changes.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;em&gt;“Improve my cache hit rate”&lt;/em&gt; the skill applies prompt caching rules that many developers miss, helping reduce costs on Bedrock.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;em&gt;“Add context compaction to my agent”&lt;/em&gt; it walks you through compaction primitives and agent patterns for long-running Fable 5.2 sessions.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The claude-api skill is available in Claude Code and is also bundled into CodeRabbit, JetBrains IDEs (including Junie), Resolve AI, and Warp. It is open source at &lt;a href="https://github.com/anthropics/skills/tree/main/skills/claude-api" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;anthropics/skills&lt;/a&gt; on GitHub, so any coding agent can integrate it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="availability"&gt;Availability&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Claude Fable 5.2 and Claude Mythos 5.2 are available today on Amazon Bedrock through US Geo CRIS and Global CRIS inference profiles. See the&lt;u&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/model-cards-anthropic.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"&gt; Bedrock documentation&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/u&gt; for the full list of supported Regions. For pricing information, see&lt;u&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"&gt; Amazon Bedrock pricing&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/u&gt;. Both models are also available through Claude Platform on AWS in every Region where the service is offered.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Give Claude Fable 5.2 a try in the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/bedrock" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock console&lt;/a&gt;, in &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/claude-platform/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Claude Platform on AWS&lt;/a&gt;, or explore the &lt;u&gt;&lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/anthropic-on-aws/blob/main/notebooks/claude_fable_5_2_getting_started/claude-fable-5-2-getting-started.ipynb" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"&gt;Getting Started notebook&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/u&gt; on GitHub.&lt;/p&gt;

		<item>
		<title>NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL is now available in Amazon SageMaker JumpStart</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/nvidia-nemotron-3-5-super-vl-is-now-available-in-amazon-sagemaker-jumpstart/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Venu Kanamatareddy]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Mon, 09 Nov 2026 20:01:20 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon SageMaker JumpStart]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Announcements]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">3e5b07e97d0a4f1f703ec7e89ab19f1b40aaa262</guid>

					<description>NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL, a 120B-parameter multimodal vision-language model, is now available in Amazon SageMaker JumpStart. Deploy the NVFP4 or BF16 variant in a few steps, then run text, image, and video inference for document intelligence, computer-use agents, and multimodal agentic workloads.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Today, we’re announcing that NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL is available in Amazon SageMaker JumpStart. You can deploy it with a few steps in SageMaker Studio or with a few lines of the SageMaker Python SDK. Super VL is the vision-language model (VLM) in the NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 family. It accepts images, video, and text as input and generates text.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The model pairs a 120B-parameter hybrid Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language backbone with 12B active parameters and a C-RADIO vision encoder. It supports context lengths of up to 1 million tokens and includes a built-in Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) head that supports speculative decoding. You can configure reasoning per request.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;NVIDIA built Super VL for document intelligence, GUI understanding for computer-use agents, video question answering, and multimodal agentic applications. JumpStart offers two variants: NVFP4, quantized from BF16 with quantization-aware distillation for optimized inference, and the BF16 full-precision reference weights. In this post, we walk through the model’s capabilities and the JumpStart deployment configurations. We then show the measured inference performance and demonstrate text, image, and video inference.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="model-overview"&gt;Model overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Nemotron 3.5 Super VL is a 120B-parameter model with 12B active parameters per token. It combines a hybrid language backbone with a dedicated vision encoder.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Language backbone&lt;/strong&gt;: 88 layers that interleave Mamba-2 and MoE layers (512 routed experts, 22 active per token) with 8 attention layers, plus a shared MTP head. The backbone is initialized from Nemotron 3 Super Base and distilled from Nemotron 3 Ultra Base.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Vision encoder&lt;/strong&gt;: A C-RADIO vision encoder encodes images and sampled video frames, and a vision-language connector maps them into the language backbone’s embedding space. The encoder and connector stay in BF16 in both variants.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Training&lt;/strong&gt;: The model first goes through continued pre-training, long-context and software-engineering mid-training, and vision-language training. It then goes through two-stage multimodal supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on vision, reasoning, code, tool, chat, safety, and long-context data. Next come reinforcement learning (RL) with verifiable rewards and human feedback in NeMo RL and NeMo Gym, multi-teacher on-policy distillation, and a final stage that further trains the MTP head. The NVFP4 checkpoint is produced from BF16 with NVIDIA Model Optimizer and quantization-aware distillation. It keeps the vision encoder, connector, Mamba convolutions, and embeddings in BF16 and the MoE router in FP32. It runs with 4-bit weights and activations (W4A4) on Blackwell GPUs.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following table compares the specifications of the two variants available in JumpStart.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Specification&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;NVFP4&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;BF16&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Parameters&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;120B total, 12B active&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;120B total, 12B active&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Intended for&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Optimized inference (latency and throughput)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Full-precision reference weights for customization: SFT, RL, distillation, domain adaptation, and custom quantization&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Inputs / output&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Image, video, text / text&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Image, video, text / text&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Context length&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Up to 1M tokens (JumpStart serving configurations use 520 to 15,600 tokens)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Checkpoint size&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;About 75 GB&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;About 249 GB&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;JumpStart instances&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ml.g7.24xlarge (default), ml.g7.48xlarge&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ml.p5en.48xlarge&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Speculative decoding&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Built-in MTP head. Not enabled in the JumpStart serving configuration&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;LoRA adapter serving&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;No&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Languages&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;English, French, German, Italian, Japanese, Spanish, Chinese&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Reasoning&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;On by default. Configurable with &lt;code&gt;enable_thinking&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Outputs&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Tool calling, JSON schema output, configurable reasoning&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;License&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;a href="https://openmdw.ai/license/1-1/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenMDW License Agreement, version 1.1&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Same&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="key-use-cases"&gt;Key use cases&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Super VL is aimed at agentic workloads that must see and read. It targets vision-language agents that combine reasoning, coding, tool use, document understanding, and video understanding.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Document intelligence&lt;/strong&gt;: Optical character recognition (OCR), charts, tables, and multipage documents. Each 1,536 x 1,024 page image costs about 1,536 tokens of context. The JumpStart serving configuration accepts one image and one video per request.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;GUI understanding&lt;/strong&gt;: Screen grounding and reasoning for computer-use agents that navigate applications.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Video understanding&lt;/strong&gt;: Analyze and summarize short MP4 video clips, including answering questions about visual content. You can provide videos inline as base64-encoded content, subject to the endpoint’s request payload size limits.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Multimodal agents&lt;/strong&gt;: Tool calling, JSON schema output through &lt;code&gt;response_format&lt;/code&gt;, and configurable reasoning support multimodal agent workflows.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Customization (BF16)&lt;/strong&gt;: Download the BF16 reference weights for SFT, RL, distillation, domain adaptation, or creating your own quantized variants. The JumpStart endpoint serves the base weights only and does not load LoRA adapters.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="model-quality-benchmarks"&gt;Model-quality benchmarks&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The NVIDIA model cards report results for both checkpoints across reasoning, coding, agentic, long-context, and multimodal benchmarks, with evaluation settings held constant so that only the checkpoint differs. The following table highlights the multimodal benchmarks most relevant to vision-language workloads (reasoning on).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Benchmark&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Capability&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;BF16&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;NVFP4&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;MMLongBench-Doc&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Long-document multimodal understanding&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;62.82&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;62.82&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;OCRBench v2, EN&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;OCR and document understanding&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;68.12&lt;/td&gt;

   &lt;td&gt;52.30&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;NVFP4, the JumpStart default, matches BF16 on MMLongBench-Doc and stays within about one point on the others, scoring slightly higher on OCRBench v2 and OSWorld-Verified. For the full comparison against other publicly available models and the evaluation settings, see the &lt;a href="https://research.nvidia.com/labs/nemotron/files/NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Super-and-Lightning-Technical-Report.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL and Lightning Technical Report&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="inference-performance-on-sagemaker-jumpstart"&gt;Inference performance on SageMaker JumpStart&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We measured inference performance with the JumpStart release container using the generate workload profile: 250 input tokens, 1,500 output tokens, and five concurrent streaming requests. Throughput is the mean output tokens per second for each request, and time to first token is the mean across requests.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Instance type&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Variant&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Output throughput per request (mean)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Time to first token (mean)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ml.g7.24xlarge&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;NVFP4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;63.07 tokens/sec&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0.45 sec&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ml.g7.48xlarge&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;NVFP4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;64.01 tokens/sec&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0.51 sec&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ml.p5en.48xlarge&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;BF16&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;140.85 tokens/sec&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;0.53 sec&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These results illustrate the deployment choices available through JumpStart. The NVFP4 variant runs on G7 instances with a smaller GPU footprint. The BF16 variant on ml.p5en.48xlarge achieved 140.85 output tokens per second per request in this test. Time to first token remained about half a second across all three configurations. Because the variants run on different GPU systems and configurations, these measurements are not an isolated &lt;code&gt;BF16-versus-NVFP4&lt;/code&gt; precision comparison. Longer prompts change the picture: with the summarize profile (10,000 input tokens), time to first token on ml.g7.24xlarge is 12.62 seconds.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="supported-instances"&gt;Supported instances&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;SageMaker JumpStart supports Nemotron 3.5 Super VL on the following validated instance types. NVFP4 runs on G7 instances with NVIDIA RTX PRO 4500 Blackwell Server Edition GPUs, and BF16 runs on P5en instances with NVIDIA H200 GPUs. The JumpStart default instance for NVFP4 is ml.g7.24xlarge, which serves as the recommended starting point for the generate profile.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Variant&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;

   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;GPUs&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;GPU memory&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Tensor parallel&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;NVFP4&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ml.g7.24xlarge (default)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;4x NVIDIA RTX PRO 4500 Blackwell Server Edition&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;128 GB&lt;/td&gt;

  &lt;/tr&gt;

   &lt;td&gt;8&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The checkpoint includes the MTP head, but the JumpStart serving configuration does not enable speculative decoding.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="deploy-nemotron-3.5-super-vl-with-sagemaker-jumpstart"&gt;Deploy Nemotron 3.5 Super VL with SageMaker JumpStart&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can deploy the model from SageMaker Studio, with the SageMaker Python SDK, or from the Hugging Face model page.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before you deploy, make sure you have the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with permissions to create Amazon SageMaker AI endpoints.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) role with access to SageMaker AI.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Service quota for ml.g7.24xlarge, ml.g7.48xlarge, or ml.p5en.48xlarge endpoint usage. The ml.g7 quotas start at zero in new accounts, so request an increase through Service Quotas before you deploy.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For the SDK path, the SageMaker Python SDK v3 (version 3.25 or later).&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="deploy-from-sagemaker-studio"&gt;Deploy from SageMaker Studio&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To deploy the model from SageMaker Studio, complete the following steps:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Open SageMaker Studio and, in the navigation pane, choose &lt;strong&gt;JumpStart&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the search bar, enter Nemotron 3.5 Super VL. The search results list both variants, as shown in the following screenshot. &lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
   &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-1.png" alt="SageMaker JumpStart search results listing the two NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL variants" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
   &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Searching for NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL in SageMaker JumpStart&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose the model card for the variant you want to deploy. Choose NVFP4 for optimized inference on G7 instances. The following screenshot shows the NVFP4 model card. 
  &lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
   &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-2.png" alt="NVFP4 model card for NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL in SageMaker JumpStart" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
   &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL NVFP4 model card in SageMaker JumpStart&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt; &lt;p&gt;Choose BF16 if you need the full-precision reference weights on ml.p5en.48xlarge. The following screenshot shows the BF16 model card.&lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
   &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-3.png" alt="BF16 model card for NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL in SageMaker JumpStart" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
   &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL BF16 model card in SageMaker JumpStart&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the model card, review the model details and license terms, then choose &lt;strong&gt;Deploy&lt;/strong&gt;. The following screenshot shows the model details you can review before deployment. &lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
   &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-4.png" alt="Model details and capabilities page for Nemotron 3.5 Super VL before deployment" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
   &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: Reviewing Nemotron 3.5 Super VL capabilities and model details before deployment&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;For &lt;strong&gt;Instance type&lt;/strong&gt;, keep the default (ml.g7.24xlarge for NVFP4 or ml.p5en.48xlarge for BF16), or choose another supported instance type. Then choose &lt;strong&gt;Deploy&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Wait for the endpoint status to change to InService before you invoke the model. Deployment takes about 10 minutes on ml.g7.24xlarge. The following screenshot shows a deployment in progress. &lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
   &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-5.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-5.png" alt="SageMaker endpoint deployment in progress before the status reaches InService" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
   &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: SageMaker endpoint deployment in progress before the status reaches InService&lt;/p&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="deploy-with-the-sagemaker-python-sdk"&gt;Deploy with the SageMaker Python SDK&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;# Install the SageMaker Python SDK v3: pip install "sagemaker&amp;gt;=3.25"
from sagemaker.core.helper.session_helper import get_execution_role
from sagemaker.core.jumpstart.configs import JumpStartConfig
from sagemaker.core.training.configs import Compute
from sagemaker.serve import ModelBuilder

# BF16 variant: model_id "huggingface-vlm-nvidia-nemotron-3-5-super-vl-120b-a12b-bf16" on ml.p5en.48xlarge.
jumpstart_config = JumpStartConfig(
    model_id="huggingface-vlm-nvidia-nemotron-3-5-super-vl-120b-a12b-nvfp4",
    inference_config_name="modify_best_price_performance",
)
model_builder = ModelBuilder.from_jumpstart_config(
    jumpstart_config=jumpstart_config,
    role_arn=get_execution_role(),
    compute=Compute(instance_type="ml.g7.24xlarge", instance_count=1),
)
model_builder.build()
endpoint = model_builder.deploy(endpoint_name="nemotron-3-5-super-vl")&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The example uses the SageMaker Python SDK v3 classes: &lt;code&gt;JumpStartConfig&lt;/code&gt; names the model, &lt;code&gt;ModelBuilder&lt;/code&gt; builds and deploys it, and &lt;code&gt;deploy&lt;/code&gt; returns an &lt;code&gt;Endpoint&lt;/code&gt; resource. Pass the instance type in &lt;code&gt;Compute&lt;/code&gt;, because the SDK falls back to ml.m5.large when the instance type is absent, and the build then fails. The deploy call waits until the endpoint is InService, which takes about 10 minutes on ml.g7.24xlarge.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;inference_config_name&lt;/code&gt; argument selects a serving configuration, and each configuration sets a different context length. The default generate configurations serve a 2,275-token context sized for short prompts. The summarize configurations serve 13,650 tokens, and the modify configurations serve 15,600 tokens. The example uses a modify configuration because the inference examples in this post, especially image, video, and reasoning requests, need the larger context. All the inference examples that follow assume the modify configuration.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="deploy-from-hugging-face"&gt;Deploy from Hugging Face&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can also deploy the model from its Hugging Face model page: choose &lt;strong&gt;Deploy&lt;/strong&gt;, then &lt;strong&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/strong&gt;, and continue to the SageMaker deployment workflow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-6.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-6.png" alt="Hugging Face model page for NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL with the Deploy menu open" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL model page on Hugging Face with the Deploy menu&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On the deployment menu, choose &lt;strong&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/strong&gt; to open the SageMaker deployment workflow, as shown in the following screenshot.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-7.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/04/ML-22096-7.png" alt="Amazon SageMaker AI option selected in the Hugging Face deployment workflow" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 7: Selecting Amazon SageMaker AI from the Hugging Face deployment workflow&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After the endpoint is InService, you can send inference requests to it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="run-inference"&gt;Run inference&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The JumpStart endpoint exposes a vLLM-powered chat completions API, so applications can send inference requests using a familiar chat-message format. NVIDIA recommends temperature 1.0 and &lt;code&gt;top_p&lt;/code&gt; 0.95 for text, and temperature 0.6 and &lt;code&gt;top_p&lt;/code&gt; 0.95 for image and video requests. You must send media inline as base64 data URLs because the endpoint runs with network isolation and cannot fetch URLs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="text-with-reasoning-on"&gt;Text, with reasoning on&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Reasoning is on by default, and you can configure it per request. The reasoning text is returned in the reasoning field of the message, separate from content.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import json

payload = {
    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content":
        "Explain the difference between OCR and document understanding."}],
    "max_tokens": 2000,
    "temperature": 1.0,
    "top_p": 0.95,
}
response = endpoint.invoke(
    body=json.dumps(payload), content_type="application/json", accept="application/json"
)
result = json.loads(response.body.read())
print(result["choices"][0]["message"]["reasoning"])
print(result["choices"][0]["message"]["content"])&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="image-with-reasoning-off"&gt;Image, with reasoning off&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For direct extraction-style tasks, you can disable reasoning with &lt;code&gt;enable_thinking=False&lt;/code&gt; in &lt;code&gt;chat_template_kwargs&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import base64

with open("invoice.png", "rb") as f:
    image_b64 = base64.b64encode(f.read()).decode("utf-8")

payload = {
    "messages": [{
        "role": "user",
        "content": [
            {"type": "image_url",
             "image_url": {"url": f"data:image/png;base64,{image_b64}"}},
            {"type": "text",
             "text": "Extract the vendor, date, and total as JSON."},
        ],
    }],
    "max_tokens": 1024,
    "temperature": 0.6,
    "top_p": 0.95,
    "chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": False},
}
response = endpoint.invoke(
    body=json.dumps(payload), content_type="application/json", accept="application/json"
)
result = json.loads(response.body.read())
print(result["choices"][0]["message"]["content"])&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The model accepts images rather than raw PDF files. To process a PDF, render each page to an image such as PNG before sending it to the model. A 1,536 x 1,024 page costs about 1,536 tokens of context, and one request can carry one image.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="video"&gt;Video&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following example sends a short MP4 clip as a base64-encoded data URL and asks the model to summarize it, with reasoning disabled for a direct response.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import base64

with open("demo.mp4", "rb") as f:
    video_b64 = base64.b64encode(f.read()).decode("utf-8")

payload = {
    "messages": [{
        "role": "user",
        "content": [
            {"type": "video_url",
             "video_url": {"url": f"data:video/mp4;base64,{video_b64}"}},
            {"type": "text", "text": "Summarize what happens in this video."},
        ],
    }],
    "max_tokens": 2000,
    "temperature": 0.6,
    "top_p": 0.95,
    "chat_template_kwargs": {"enable_thinking": False},
}
response = endpoint.invoke(
    body=json.dumps(payload), content_type="application/json", accept="application/json"
)
result = json.loads(response.body.read())
print(result["choices"][0]["message"]["content"])&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For video inference, use short, compressed clips and keep the encoded request within the endpoint’s supported payload limits. For reasoning-intensive video requests, set &lt;code&gt;max_tokens&lt;/code&gt; high enough to allow the model to complete its reasoning while considering response length and endpoint invocation limits.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="best-practices"&gt;Best practices&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Consider the following recommendations when you run Nemotron 3.5 Super VL on SageMaker JumpStart:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Media inputs&lt;/strong&gt;: The model accepts images rather than raw PDF files, so render PDF pages to images first.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Video configuration&lt;/strong&gt;: Frame sampling is set when the endpoint launches, not per request. The JumpStart endpoint uses the vLLM default sampling and rejects per-request &lt;code&gt;media_io_kwargs&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Video with reasoning&lt;/strong&gt;: Set &lt;code&gt;max_tokens&lt;/code&gt; to at least 8,192 for video with reasoning enabled, or disable reasoning when you want a direct response.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Context budget&lt;/strong&gt;: &lt;code&gt;max_tokens&lt;/code&gt; caps the completion. Prompt and completion together must fit in the serving configuration’s model length: 2,275 tokens for the generate configurations, 520 for interact, 13,650 for summarize, and 15,600 for modify. Images and video frames also consume context.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Multipage documents&lt;/strong&gt;: The JumpStart serving configuration accepts one image per request. Send one page per request, or stitch pages into one image, and check the context budget.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Reasoning mode&lt;/strong&gt;: Use reasoning for multi-step analysis and agentic tasks. Disable it for extraction or captioning when you want a shorter, direct response.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Sampling&lt;/strong&gt;: Start with temperature 1.0 and &lt;code&gt;top_p&lt;/code&gt; 0.95 for text, and temperature 0.6 and &lt;code&gt;top_p&lt;/code&gt; 0.95 for image and video, as NVIDIA recommends.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, delete the endpoint, the endpoint configuration, and the model when you are done:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from sagemaker.core.resources import EndpointConfig, Model

endpoint_config = EndpointConfig.get(endpoint_config_name=endpoint.endpoint_config_name)
model_name = endpoint_config.production_variants[0].model_name
endpoint.delete()
endpoint_config.delete()
Model.get(model_name=model_name).delete()&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Super VL brings image, video, and document understanding to SageMaker JumpStart. The NVFP4 variant is available on G7 instances with NVIDIA RTX PRO 4500 Blackwell Server Edition GPUs. The BF16 variant is available on ml.p5en.48xlarge with NVIDIA H200 GPUs. Choose NVFP4 for optimized inference with model quality close to the BF16 checkpoint, or BF16 when you want the full-precision reference weights. To get started, open SageMaker JumpStart in SageMaker Studio or use the SageMaker Python SDK.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To learn more, see SageMaker JumpStart pretrained models in the Amazon SageMaker AI Developer Guide and the &lt;a href="https://research.nvidia.com/labs/nemotron/files/NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-Super-and-Lightning-Technical-Report.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Nemotron 3.5 Super VL technical report&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;
